みずほ経済 EXPRESS

みずほ GDP ナウ（2026 年 10 月中旬時点） 
～7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.03％（年率＋0.10％）と推計～

調査部  シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山祐資 
同             エコノミスト  阿部大樹 
080-1069-4835 yusuke.koshiyama@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 10 月中旬時点の「みずほ GDP ナウ」は 7～9 月期 GDP を前期比＋0.10％と予測 
みずほ総合研究所では景気動向をタイムリーに把握するべく、浦沢（2023）等を参考にGDPナウキ
ャスティングに取り組んできた。これを「みずほGDPナウ」と呼称することとし、太田他（2024）で
はデータドリブン予測である「みずほGDPナウ」が民間予測平均並みの予測精度を確保できることを
示した。その上で、酒井他（2024）をはじめとして、月次経済指標を用いたGDPナウキャスティング
の結果を紹介してきたところである。 
本稿では10月中旬時点までに得られる月次経済指標を用い、 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期GDP
の予測結果を紹介する。予測に用いたデータは、8月分の鉱工業生産、 消費活動指数、 所定外労働時間、
消費財出荷指数、第3次産業活動指数、9月分の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見通しDI）である1。図表1
には、予測に使用した各月次経済指標の推移を示している。 
図表 1 各月次経済指標の推移

### Page 2

2

モデルによる10月中旬時点における7 ～9月期実質GDP成長率の予測値は、図表2のとおり前期比＋
0.03％ （年率＋0.10％）となった。 他機関による予測では、東京財団政策研究所による10月15日時点の
ナウキャスティングが同＋0.29％（年率＋1.18％）、日本経済研究センターが公表した10月のESPフォ
ーキャスト調査（回答期間：2026年9月30日～10月7日、回答者37名）における民間予測値平均が前期
比年率＋0.08％となっている。「みずほGDPナウ」の予測値は、民間コンセンサスよりやや高いもの
の、概ね近い伸びを予測していると言える。 
なお、多くの民間エコノミストが保守的な見方をしている理由の一つには、GDP統計作成上のテク
ニカルな要因がある。4～6月期には、 中東情勢悪化を背景に原油 ・ ナフサ輸入が大幅に減少したほか、
石油化学製品の供給途絶懸念から幅広い企業で在庫積み増しの動きが生じた。やや直感に反するが、
いずれもGDPに対しては押し上げ要因となる。 そのため、4～6月期GDPは前期比＋0.36％ （年率＋1.45％）
とプラス成長になったものの、 成長率が示すほど景気の実態は強くなかったとみるのが自然であろう。
反対に、7～9月期については、4～6月期の反動で輸入増・在庫減の動きが顕在化し、GDPには一定の
下方圧力がかかる可能性がある。みずほ総合研究所が9月に公表したレポート2でも、こうしたテクニ
カル要因を考慮の上、7～9月期GDPは横ばい圏の伸びにとどまると予想している。 
■ 「みずほ GDP ナウ」による予測値下方修正の主因は生産・消費関連指標の悪化 
「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期のGDP（1次速報値） の予測にあたっては10月中旬までに公表さ
れた月次経済指標を使って予測値を4回更新した。図表3では、予測値の更新過程と各月次経済指標の
寄与度を示している3。予測値が下方修正された主因は、8月の鉱工業生産、 消費財出荷指数の低下だ。
特に鉱工業生産指数については、7月末に発生した熊本地震による影響が下押し要因になったとみら
れる。 大手自動車メーカーで工場の操業が停止した影響で自動車が前月比▲6.8％と大幅に減産したほ
か、 電子部品 ・ デバイスも同▲1.9％の減産となった。7月調査時点の生産予測指数は前月比＋3.1％（ 補
図表 2 各機関の実質GDP成長率予測値 図表 3 予測値の改定過程と各経済指標の寄与度

ト調査」等より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 3

3

正値ベース）と増産が見込まれていたが、大幅に下振れた格好だ。一方、世界的に旺盛なAI・データ
センター関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置を含む生産用機械などが増産したものの、熊本地震の影
響を打ち返せなかった。消費財出荷指数の低下については、4～5月の耐久財特需が一巡したことで反
動減の動きが生じた。非耐久財の出荷は前月から増加したものの、7月の落ち込みも踏まえると、 基調
としては力強さを欠いていると言えよう。長雨や低温、といった天候不順により生鮮野菜価格が上昇
しているほか、食肉も値上がりが大きく、こうした手に取る機会が多い品目の価格上昇が家計の節約
志向を強め、個人消費の下押し圧力となっているとみられる。9月についても、食品のほか、原油・石
油化学製品等のコスト高の影響で幅広い品目の価格上昇が加速する見込みであり、個人消費は冴えな
い動きが続く可能性がある。 
以上のように、7～9月期成長率は低い伸びに抑えられる公算が大きい。前述のとおり原油・ナフサ
の輸入増、 企業の在庫調整といった動きはGDP統計上、成長率に対してマイナスに寄与する。加えて、
熊本地震による生産減や天候要因による消費支出抑制の動きが下押し要因になる見込みだ。 
次回の 「みずほGDPナウ」については、11月16日に公表される7～9月期GDP1次速報を挟んで、10～
12月期GDPのリアルタイム予測に取り組む。10月分の鉱工業生産や消費活動指数の結果等を踏まえて
推計し、12月中旬頃のレポート発刊を予定している。原油高等を受けた物価上昇圧力の強まりや堅調
なAI・データセンター関連需要など、プラス・マイナス双方の影響が交錯する中、ナウキャストによ
る景気動向の把握はより重要なものとなるだろう。

1 ステップワイズ法により使用データを採択した。詳細は太田ほか（2024）を参照されたい。今後のモデルの予測精度のパフォ
ーマンス評価等を踏まえ、採択するデータについては見直しを行う可能性がある。 
2 詳細については、服部直樹「年率＋1.4％と 1 次速報から小幅に上方修正（4～6 月期2 次QE）」（2026 年9 月8 日）を参照され
たい。 
3 太田他（2024）で示した枠組みと同様、図表 2 の折れ線が各時点における実質GDP 成長率の予測値であり、月次の経済指標が
新たに公表されたり改定されたりすることで予測値が更新される。棒グラフは、予測値の改定幅、すなわち前回予測との差を各
月次経済指標で寄与度分解したもので、寄与度を合計するとモデル予測値の改定幅と一致する。なお、 「みずほGDP ナウ」は各
月次経済指標についても公表前に予測値を計算しており、各指標の寄与度はモデル予測値と実績値の乖離から計算されることか
ら、寄与度の符号は経済指標の変化の方向とは必ずしも一致しない。

金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 30 日
上週重點訊息
◼ 美國企業投資動能維持韌性 ：8 月標普全球美國綜合 PMI 升至 58.4，創2021 年 7 月來高點 ，
製造業、服務業同步擴張至 57 和 58.7； 8 月扣除飛機與國防的核心資本財訂單月增 1.6%，
高於前月修正後的 0.6%。其中，初級金屬、機械及電機設備訂單均呈現成長，顯示企業資本
支出、AI 投資與國防需求仍具韌性，持續支撐美國經濟成長。
◼ 美中同意貿易休兵再延長兩個月：美中同意調降雙邊總值 600 億美元商品的關稅，涵蓋美國

玉米、化妝品及中國家電、玩具等產品，並延長貿易休兵至明年 1 月 10 日，同時中國承諾繼
續供應稀土並擴大採購美國農產品與煤礦，美國則繼續暫停實施 50%穿透性規則；AI 方面，
美中雙方雖未就 AI 監管議題達成共識 ， 但同意建立風險溝通機制 ， 並於11 月底前展開對話 。
◼ Meta 旗下 AI 代理人 Muse 登頂排行榜：Meta 新款個人 AI 代理 Muse 上線後迅速累積逾
250 萬次下載，並登上美國 App Store 下載排行榜首位。相較過去生成式 AI 以問答與內容生
成為主，Muse 可進一步協助安排日程、搜尋資訊、處理文件及完成交易，代表 AI 正從提升
效率工具逐步走向數位勞動力，市場評價焦點也將逐漸由 AI 基礎設施擴散至應用軟體業者。
市場展望
◼ AI 需求持續擴張，科技供應鏈成長動能延續：AI 基礎建設需求持續擴張，帶動 HBM、伺服器
DRAM、 先進製程與封裝等關鍵供應鏈維持成長動能 ， 市場表現由全面上漲逐步轉向結構性分
化。若 AI 伺服器需求持續成長且 CSP 資本支出維持高檔，具供給優勢、定價能力及較高訂單

能見度之企業，其營運表現有機會獲得支撐。整體而言，科技產業獲利表現仍具關注度，惟後
續仍須留意全球景氣、產業供需變化、政策監管及市場評價調整等風險因素。
◼ 債券佈局可關注中天期美國公債及投等債：中天期美國公債及投資級債仍值得持續關注：**雖
然貨幣政策風險下降有助於穩定長期通膨預期 ， 但政府與企業發債供給增加所帶來的排擠效應 ，
以及美國經濟成長展現韌性，仍可能使長天期利率面臨上行壓力。因此，在當前利率環境下，
中天期債券於票息收益與存續期間風險之間相對較為均衡。投資級債方面，彭博美國投資級公
司債指數利差約 80 個基點，雖然利差偏窄，但高基準利率環境仍提供相對較高的收益水準。
因此，在目前市場環境下，中天期美國公債及投資級債仍為值得持續觀察的債券資產類別，惟
後續表現仍須留意利率走勢、信用利差變化及經濟成長動能等因素。

## Document 106: 261013_ubs_alchip

Source PDF: all report pdf/261013_ubs_alchip.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
First Read
Alchip Technologies
Better allocation into 2027E; N2 project on track
Q3 sales up 243% QoQ on Trainium 3 ramp
Alchip reported September sales of NT$9,979 mn, up 14% MoM and 496% YoY,
concluding Q326 sales of NT$26,188 mn for +243% QoQ and 299% YoY, slightly
ahead of consensus, although below UBSe as we assumed a higher wafer allocation for
the Trainium 3 project, while sales contribution for its Chinese automotive customer and

NRE might also have came in lower than our expectation. We now expect Alchip's Q326
GM to arrive at 20.1%, down from 34.8% in Q226 on less favourable mix, leading to
OPM of 15.9%. We forecast Q326 EPS to grow 132% QoQ to NT$46.34.
Trainium 3  to sustain longer with potential upside; Trainium 4 tape-out on track
We expect Alchip's Trainium 3 monthly shipment run-rate to retain at current level into
H127F, and could extend into H227F with potential better allocation for Alchip with a

similar margin profile, subject to the final negotiation with its customer. We think
Trainium 3 deployments could stretch a little bit longer with a limited gap (vs prior ~3
months) before the next-generation Trainium 4 launch given the 3nm capacity tightness.
We continue to forecast overall Trainium 3 shipments at ~1.8mn units for 2026 and will
increase to ~2.8 mn units in 2027E, up 55% YoY, although Alchip's Trainium 3 shipment
could outgrow at 80%+ YoY given potential better allocation. For the Trainium 4 project

(2nm node), management noted it remains on track for tape-out before year-end and
still targets mass production by late-2027 or early 2028. Alchip continues to believe it
could enjoy a higher value-add given the increasing design complexity on chiplet and
higher wafer pricing for the 2nm node, while GM could retain at a similar level
compared with the current N3 project.
Engaging new customers for diversification
Alchip noted it remains in talks with another major CSP customer for potential adoption

of COT (customer-owned tooling) business model in the future for further cost saving.
We believe Alchip's competitive pricing,  strength for faster turnaround, and advanced
packaging knowledge put Alchip in a  good position to capture this opportunity, if the
customer decides to change the business model. Nevertheless, we do not expect
meaningful revenue contribution in 2027-28E from the new CSP customer. On the other
hand, we believe Alchip is in talks with other HPC customers for mid-size physical design

projects and this is likely to become a new revenue stream from 2027E. We estimate it
could book US$200-300mn revenue in 2027E from the new HPC customer.
Valuation: Maintain Buy with NT$6,600 price target
We lower our 2026E EPS by 11% primarily on lower Trainium 3 and automotive
contribution. We raise 2027E/28E EPS by 2%/5% to reflect  revenue contribution from
new HPC customer, offset by higher opex. We keep our PT unchanged at NT$6,600,
based on the same 30x 2027E EPS (vs. a 15-50x PE range). Stock is trading at ~20x 2027

P/E, below its peers and past five year average of 30x.

Company Description
Alchip was founded in 2002 by a group of Cadence managers with 20+ years of experience in
the semiconductor industry. It is an IC design and production service provider, and
management believes it can leverage third-party IP to enable customized chipset solutions for

customers cost effectively and fast to market. The company was funded by Cisco Investment
and Softbank Investment with VC funding in early 2000, then had its IPO on the emerging
board in Taiwan in 2010 and the main board in 2014.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value Alchip using PE methodology.
Downside risks include slower-than-expected advanced node project diversification and
ramp, and growing competition in advanced projects from other IC design service companies.

horizon of 12 months.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

## Document 139: 261020_ml_nienmade

Source PDF: all report pdf/261020_ml_nienmade.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Nien Made Enterprise

3Q preview: EPS +13% YoY to NT$6.6; 
Better 2H but not yet a turnaround

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 441.00 TWD | Price: 358.50 TWD

3Qe EPS NT$6.6: strong rev and GP offset by opex and FX 
Nien Made is scheduled to report 3Q26 results on 4 Nov. Disclosed monthly sales imply 
3Q26 revenue growth of 16% YoY to NT$8.5bn, or 9% in USD terms, largely 
volume-driven. Growth reaccelerated from 1.7% YoY in 1H26, led by custom-made on a 
low US base and expansion in Europe and Japan. Ready-made also improved as 
Cambodia labor shortages eased. (See Exhibit 1 for US window coverings spending 
trends). Gross margin: We expect underlying GPM to rise 1.1ppt YoY to 59.8% on lower 
tariffs, broadly flat QoQ. We forecasts an NT$300mn of IEEPA tariff refunds in 2H26. 
Assuming NT$150mn is booked in 3Q, this would add 1.7ppt and lift reported GPM to 
61.5%. We expect the selling expense ratio to fall 1.7ppt QoQ to 17.5% on lower fuel 
charges, but remain 0.5ppt higher YoY due to continued salesforce and distribution 
network investment. We expect an FX loss in 3Q26 vs. an NT$118mn gain in 3Q25, 
weighing on YoY earnings growth. We forecast 3Q26 EPS of NT$6.6, up 13% YoY. 
US window covering picking up, but no turnaround yet  
BofA credit/debit card data suggests US spending on window coverings turned positive 
in Aug-26 for the first time after 19 consecutive months of YoY declines. Spending rose 
4%/3% YoY in Aug/Sep, respectively. This improvement partly explains the recent 
reacceleration in Nien Made's monthly sales. That said, we view the improvement more 
as normalization off a low base than the start of an industry turnaround. The Aug-Sep 
2025 comparison base was relatively weak, while the current rate-hike environment 
remains a headwind to housing-related demand. 
Slightly raise FY26 EPS est. to NT$22.9; PO to NT$441 
We raise our FY26 EPS estimate by 2% to NT$22.9 to reflect the blended impact of 
stronger-than-expected revenue growth and FX loss. We maintain our FY26 DPS forecast 
of NT$17 per share. We raise our PO by 4% to NT$441, as we roll forward our valuation 
basis from FY26E EPS to FY27E EPS. At the same time, we lower our target P/E from 
19x to 17x, reflecting the MSCI removal and a less supportive rate environment.

Estimates (Dec) (NT$) 2024A 2025A 2026E 2027E 2028E 
Net Income (Adjusted - mn) 6,316 7,031 6,689 7,598 8,249

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 7.

Company Sector 
Interior Furnishings

Company Description 
Nien Made, founded in 1974, is one of the world's largest 
window-covering manufacturers. It has roughly 50% 
market share in both ready-made (OEM business) shades 
and blinds, and custom-made (own brand) shutters in the 
US. The US market accounted for over 75% of sales. Key 
clients include Home Depot and Wal-Mart for ready-made 
shades and blinds. Nien Made operates its own retail 
network and e-commerce business for its custom-made 
shutters under the Norman brand name in the US and 
Europe.

Investment Rationale 
We have Buy rating on Nien Made, mainly given 1) clear 
custom-made growth profile (share gain in the US and fast-
growing DTC in Japan and Europe), 2) its largest customer 
Home Depot is gaining market share, and 3) margin 
expansion potentials backed by favorable products mix.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Nien Made Enterprise (XNRBF) 
Our PO NT$441 is derived from 17x P/E 2027E EPS. This is around 0.5SD above 
historical the one-year forward P/E, which we think is warranted given the visibility of 
the growth profile.

Downside risks are 1) weaker-than-expected Home Depot's sell-through, 2) slow 
recovery of US housing turnover, and 3) higher tariff increases on China exports to the 
US. 4) Loss of key customers.

 Measures Definitions 
Business Performance Numerator Denominator 
Return On Capital Employed NOPAT = (EBIT + Interest Income) × (1 − Tax Rate) + Goodwill Amortization Total Assets − Current Liabilities + ST Debt + Accumulated Goodwill 
Amortization 
Return On Equity Net Income Shareholders’ Equity 
Operating Margin Operating Profit Sales 
Earnings Growth Expected 5 Year CAGR From Latest Actual N/A 
Free Cash Flow Cash Flow From Operations − Total Capex N/A  
Quality of Earnings Numerator Denominator 
Cash Realization Ratio Cash Flow From Operations Net Income 
Asset Replacement Ratio Capex Depreciation 
Tax Rate Tax Charge Pre-Tax Income 
Net Debt-To-Equity Ratio Net Debt = Total Debt − Cash & Equivalents Total Equity 
Interest Cover EBIT Interest Expense  
Valuation Toolkit Numerator Denominator 
Price / Earnings Ratio Current Share Price Diluted Earnings Per Share (Basis As Specified) 
Price / Book Value Current Share Price Shareholders’ Equity / Current Basic Shares 
Dividend Yield Annualised Declared Cash Dividend Current Share Price 
Free Cash Flow Yield Cash Flow From Operations − Total Capex Market Cap = Current Share Price × Current Basic Shares 
Enterprise Value / Sales EV = Current Share Price × Current Shares + Minority Equity + Net Debt + 
Other LT Liabilities 
Sales 
EV / EBITDA Enterprise Value Basic EBIT + Depreciation + Amortization 
iQmethod SMis the set of BofA Global Research standard measures that serve to maintain global consistency under three broad headings: Business Performance, Quality of Earnings, and validations. The key features of 
iQmethod are: A consistently structured, detailed, and transparent methodology. Guidelines to maximize the effectiveness of the comparative valuation process, and to identify some common pitfalls. 
iQdatabase ®  is our real-time global research database that is sourced directly from our equity analysts’ earnings models and includes forecasted as well as historical data for income statements, balance sheets, and cash 
flow statements for companies covered by BofA Global Research. 
iQprofile SM, iQmethod SM  are service marks of Bank of America Corporation. iQdatabase ® is a registered service mark of Bank of America Corporation.

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓(02)5589-9558  (113) 金管投顧新字第 021 號 
公司拜訪快報

晟田(4541)：航太及半導體客戶需求延續，帶動 2027 年獲利成長 
建議：中立轉買進

一、晟田為航太精密機械零件加工廠 
晟田成立於 1991 年 8 月，實收資本額 6.76 億元，截至
2Q26 底，員工約 686 位。主要從事航太零組件(包括發
動機機匣與懸塊、起落架)及機械零組件(包括半導體真
空泵浦零組件、食品飲料填充包裝設備零組件、工業自
動化精密傳動零組件等產品)之製造加工與銷售等業務。
2025 年前五大客戶為漢翔(2634)、日本 E 客戶、
SLS(Safran Landing Systems)、SPP (6355.JP)及長榮
航太(2645)。2026 年前 9 個月產品別營收佔比為航太零
組件(客供料) 48%，航太零組件(自購料) 25%，半導體
設備零組件 23%，其他 4%。

二 、 夏季電價及費用認列影響獲利 ， 預估3Q26 稅後 EPS 
1.26 元 
晟田 3Q26 營收 5.6 億元(+1.9%QoQ，+30.9%YoY)，
季增主因為受惠航太客供料客戶拉貨動能成長及半導體
客戶需求提升。獲利方面，儘管產品組合持續優化，惟
受到夏季電價及籌資相關費用認列影響，預估營業利益
1.01 億元(-6%QoQ，+97.1%YoY)。假設匯率持穩，預
估稅後淨利 0.85 億元(-1%QoQ，+34.4%YoY)， 稅後EPS 
1.26 元。 
三、產品組合持續優化，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 1.3 元 
展望 4Q26，半導體方面，考量客戶需求持續強勁，有
望帶動出貨動能；航太方面，波音(BA.US) 737 系列機
型月產能有望於 2026 年底自 42 架提升至 47 架，另，
根據空中巴士(EADSY.US)規劃，2026 年預計交付 870
架，截至 Sep-26 交付達成率約 6 成，預期 4Q26 將加
速交機 ， 帶動航太零組件拉貨需求 。 整體而言 ， 預估4Q26
營收 5.62 億元(+0.5%QoQ，+20.7%YoY)。獲利方面，
隨產品組合持續優化，且無電價因素干擾，預估營業利
益 1.05 億元(+3.9%QoQ，+28.6%YoY)。 假設匯率持穩，
預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 1.3 元。 
四、需求延續，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 4.51 元 
展望後市，儘管航太供應鏈仍受原物料短缺、人力及產
能瓶頸影響，惟波音及空巴在手訂單仍維持高位，顯示
終端需求具支撐。隨供應鏈瓶頸逐步緩解，預期將帶動
航太零組件拉貨需求回升，有利晟田營運成長。半導體
方面，受惠終端客戶持續擴大資本支出，帶動相關產品
需求穩定成長。整體而言，預估 2026 年營收 21.2 億元
(+29.1%YoY)。獲利方面，隨產品組合持續優化，預估
毛利率 25.1%，稅後淨利 3.1 億元(+122.9%YoY)，稅後
EPS 4.51 元。 
展望 2027 年，考量航空引擎零組件客戶需求強勁，晟
田規劃擴充相關產能，新廠有望於 2H27 投產；半導體
方面，持續優化相關產能，以因應客戶需求成長。整體
而言，預估 2027 年營收 23.5 億元(+10.7%YoY)。獲利
方面，考量新廠投產後折舊費用增加，預估毛利率年減
至 24.8%， 稅後淨利3.3 億元(+9%YoY)， 稅後EPS 4.58
元(以增資稀釋後股本計算)。 
五、中立轉買進理由 
評價方面，以 2027 年 EPS 估算，目前 PE 約為 13X，
介於晟田歷史區間 12X~20X 下緣 ， 調升至買進評等 ， 目
標價 75 元(2026F PE 17X)。

載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀 
 2Q26 毛利率 25.87%，低於預期，主要仍是中國毛利率拖累，2Q26 
EPS 2.02 元:迅得 2Q26 EPS 2.02 元，與元富預估 2 元差異不大。
2Q26 營收 18.24 億元， +5%QOQ，+12%YOY，2Q26 毛利 率
25.87%，低於元富預估的 26.5%，主要是中國地區仍有低價競爭影
響，本業獲利達成率 99%，業外收益挹注下，2Q26 EPS 2.02 元。 
 交期拉長，2027 年預計擴產 40%，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元:載板
客戶積極擴產，載板三雄欣興、南電、景碩均為公司客戶，近年積極
布局轉戰載板市場的臻鼎亦為客戶，由於載板面積增大， 技術難度提
升，為了維持相同生產效率須提高自動化資本支出，故以過往來看，
自動化設備佔客戶資本之約為 5%，現在提升至 15%。晶圓製造及
OSAT 隨客戶台灣及海外積極擴產， 交期過去 4-6 個月現在已經拉長
到 6-8 個月，載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑
戰 100 億元， 預計2027 年將新增產能 40%，以支應客戶未來需求。 
 維持買進評等，目標價 220 元：3Q26~2027 年營收逐季增長，新產
能逐步開出， 故維持買進，預估2026 年 EPS 8.51 元，2027 年 EPS 
11.40 元(原估 10.95 元)，目標價 220 元。(2027 PER 20x)

迅得為自動化設備供應商 
 迅得為自動化設備供應商 
迅得機械成立於 1999 年，主要產品為印刷電路板、軟板、光電及半導體物流系
統之自動化解決方案，2025 年營收比重可區分為 PCB 事業部(41%)、半導體-
載板事業部 13%、半導體-晶圓事業部 12%、半導體-封裝事業部 32%，面板事
業部 2%。主要客戶包括欣興、南亞、景碩、台郡、臻鼎、日月光、台積電、友
達、群創等。

迅得九月營收 6.95 億元，+14%MOM，+31%YOY，營收達成率 104%，優於
預期。累計 3Q26 營收 19.45 億元，+6.6%QOQ，+16.7%YOY，預估 3Q26 毛
利率 27%，預估 3Q26 EPS 2.26 元。展望第四季，原估 4Q26 營收 19.63 億元，
現因 3Q26 營收優於預期，故上修 4Q26 營收至 20.42 億元，維持 4Q26 營收
+5%QOQ 之預估，故預估 4Q26 營收 20.42 億元，+5%QOQ，+26.4%YOY，
毛利率 27.5%，預估 4Q26 EPS 2.47 元。

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (1 15)金管投顧新字第 016 號         Page 3 of 5 
個股報告

載板 、 晶圓客戶 、OSAT 客戶均樂
觀，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元

載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑戰 100 億元 
迅得 1H26 營收占比為 PCB 4 1%，載板 16%，晶圓製造 16%，封裝 25%，其
他 2%。PCB 主要產品以自動上下料、物件搬運及智慧倉儲為主； IC 載板亦為
自動上下料較多，整廠規畫約佔比 60%，單機搬運 20%，倉儲 15~20%；半導
體晶圓廠則以倉儲為主，台灣部分標準倉儲較多，微型倉儲較少， 海外部分目
前未出貨標準倉儲，僅供應微型倉儲，主要是安裝時間及人力配置考量；封裝
OSAT 廠主要供應倉儲佔比 60~70%，自動化搬運約 30%。

載板客戶積極擴產，載板三雄欣興、南電、景碩均為公司客戶，近年積極布局
轉戰載板市場的臻鼎亦為客戶，以欣興資本支出來看，2026 年 340 億元，2027
年長交期採購設備訂單金額 92 億元 ， 均將投入桃園楊梅廠及光復廠 ； 景碩2026
年資本支出 140 億元(原本是 80 億元)，將投入於六廠建置及設備導入，2027
年資本支出為 160 億元(原本是 100 億元)， 擴產進度明確 。 由於載板面積增大，
技術難度提升 ，為了維持相同生產效率須提高自動化資本支出 ，故以過往來看，
自動化設備佔客戶資本之約為 5%，現在提升至 15%。

晶圓製造部分，隨 客戶台灣及海外積極擴產，因目前現有自動化廠商均無單一
廠商可獨立供足客戶所需 自動化設備量體，故每個案件 公司均有相對應配比，
以海外廠來說，考量人力成本及安裝時間公司僅提供微型倉儲為主。 OSAT 部
分，客戶拉貨持續積極，半導體業務維持樂觀。以交期來看，過去 4~6 個月現
在已經拉長到 6-8 個月，載板、晶圓客戶、OSAT 客戶均樂觀，2027 年營收挑
戰 100 億元，預計 2027 年將新增產能 40%，以支應客戶未來需求。

維持買進，目標價 220 元

維持買進評等，目標價 220 元 
3Q26~2027 年營收逐季增長 ， 新產能逐步開出 ， 故維持買進 ， 預估2026 年 EPS 
8.51 元，2027 年 EPS 11.40 元(原估 10.95 元)， 目標價220 元。(2027 PER 20x)

風險提示 
出貨不如預期，ASP 不如預期。

## Document 200: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다삼성전자(005930) 분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다분기 영업이익 100조 시대가 열렸다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-02
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기 환율 부진에도 영업이익 100조는 넘긴다 
삼성전자 26년 3분기 실적은 이전 전망치를 하회할 전망이다. 매출액은 195.2조원으
로 이전 분기 대비 13.8% 증가하고, 영업이익은 101.7조원으로 13.6% 증가할 것으
로 예상한다. 영업이익률은 52.1%로 전 분기 대비 0.1%p 하락할 전망이다. 원/달러 
환율 영향이 주요 변수로 작용한 것으로 추정한다. 2분기에 집행되지 않았던 성과급 
일부가 3분기에 반영된 영향도 있을 것으로 예상한다. DS 부문은 매출액 149.9조원, 
영업이익은 101.8조원으로 추정하고, 디스플레이 매출액 8.8조원, 영업이익 0.9조
원, MX/네트워크는 매출액 34.3조원, 영업적자 1.1조원으로 예상한다. VD/가전도 
영업적자 규모가 커질 것으로 예상한다.  
주주환원은 주가 상승을 견인할 것 
8월 21일 이후 주주환원에 대한 관심이 커졌다. 자사주 규모는 크지 않겠지만 배당
을 통한 주주환원 규모는 사상 최대 규모일 것으로 예상한다. 글로벌 대표 IT업체와 
비교해도 손색없는 수준이다. 메모리 업체의 주주환원 규모가 100조원 넘게 집행된 
적이 없어서 주가에 어떠한 영향을 미칠 지가 관전 포인트인데 긍정적 역할을 할 것
으로 기대한다. 자사주를 통한 EPS 개선은 아니지만, 안정적이고 지속적인 규모의 
주주환원은 주가를 한층 더 높은 수준에서 평가 받는 계기가 될 것으로 판단한다.  
현저한 저평가 구간, 이전 전망 유지 
삼성전자에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 메모리 공급 부족 상황이 장기화될 
것으로 전망하고, 이로 인해 수익성은 현재 수준에서 유지되고, 이익 규모는 안정적
으로 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 현저히 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 목
표주가는 460,000원을 유지한다.

### Page 4

삼성전자 (005930)

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
반도체 Check Point 
1. 차별화된 주주환원 전략. 배경은 안정적 이익 규모 
2026년에는 대대적인 규모의 주주환원이 예상된다. 100 조원 내외 수준으로 
예상하는데 상당 부분은 배당으로 집행될 것으로 추정한다. 배당성향이 25%가 유지될 
것으로 예상하기 때문이다. 자사주 매입 여력은 20조원 내외 수준일 것으로 예상한다. 
주주환원은 주가 밸류 상승에 기여할 것으로 기대한다. 일회성이 아니고 장기적으로 
높은 수준의 주주환원이 가능하다는 것은 싸이클 변동에 따른 이익의 변동성이 
낮다는 의미이기도 하다. 
2. AI 모델 개발 속도 조절. 그래도 메모리 수요를 줄일 수는 없다 
Anthropic CEO는 현재 AI 개발 속도가 지나치게 빨라서 인간의 능력을 뛰어 넘는 
임계점에 이를 가능성이 높아져서 개발 속도를 늦출 필요가 있다고 언급했다. 이를 
Meta, Google, SpaceX 수장들이 공감했다. 우선 이들의 공감으로 AI 모델의 개발 
속도가 둔화될 것으로 보고 있지 않다. 또한, 이를 가정하더라도 향후 AI 모델은 
Agent AI로 진화할 것으로 보고 있다. 이에 따른 메모리에 대한 필요성은 급격히 
증가할 것으로 기대한다. 
3. 자신감 넘치는 HBM  
HBM4가 2026년 하반기부터 주력 제품이 될 것으로 전망한다. 26 년 3분기에는 
비중이 40% 내외, 4 분기에는 60%를 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 연초 기대 대비로는 
낮은 수준이다. 삼성전자의 점유율만 보면 연초 대비 높은 수준의 점유율이 예상된다.  
HBM4 물량이 예상보다 늦게 본격화되면서 2026년 HBM 시장 규모는 이전 전망 
대비 축소될 전망이다. HBM4도 늦은 출시로 연내 생산 물량은 이전 전망치인 130억 
Gb을 하회할 것으로 예상한다.  
2027년 HBM4 가격이 최근에 확정이 되었고, 이를 통해서 DRAM 수준의 수익성 
확보가 가능할 것으로 기대한다. 수익성 발목을 잡았던 HBM은 2027년 물량부터는 
영업이익 개선의 변수가 될 것으로 전망한다.

장기적 주주환원은 
안정적 이익이 뒷받침 
Agent AI는 메모리 
수요를 증가시킨다 
HBM4 안정적 진입한 
것으로 판단

### Page 1

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

Key: 환율 가정 하향에도 27 년 이익 전망 유지. 공급 부족 심화 
Step: 글로벌 최상위 이익 창출, 주주환원. 현저한 저평가

## Document 224: AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株は上がるのか ～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株は上がるのか ～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年 10 月 6 日

1/6 
AI がサイバー攻撃を自動化する時代、サイバー株
は上がるのか 
～攻撃能力の急伸と企業業績・株価から検証する～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ AI によるサイバー攻撃の自動化・高度化が現実のものとなっている。豪政府システムへのAI
侵入事件などが示すように、AI は与えられた目的を達成する過程で、許可された範囲を超え
てシステムにアクセスしうることが明らかになった。国内でも 9 月下旬以降、幅広い業種の
企業で不正アクセスの公表が相次いでいる。 
○ AI エージェントのサイバー課題解決能力はここ数年で急速に向上しており、サイバーセキュ
リティ企業の売上高・利益も足元で好調に推移している。 
○ 一方で、高度な AI モデルの登場は既存のサイバーセキュリティ製品の陳腐化リスクへの懸
念を生み、関連株が下落するというパラドックスも起きている。「サイバー株なら何でも上
がる」という安易な連想買いには注意が必要である。 
○ 個人投資家は、サイバーセキュリティセクター全体を丸ごと買うのではなく、「AIネイティ
ブ」なサイバーセキュリティ企業や、AIを活用した防衛技術を持つ巨大テック企業（メガテ
ック）への選別投資が求められる。

1. 豪政府システムへの「AI侵入事件」が鳴らす警鐘 
2026 年9月、オーストラリアのアルバニージー首相は、同国の公的医療保険制度（Medicare）
の統計ポータルサイトに、OpenAIのAIエージェントが無断で侵入していたと発表した。注目すべ
きは、これが悪意あるハッカーによるサイバー攻撃ではなく、OpenAIの研究チームがAIに調査を
実行させる過程で起きた点だ。AIは調査中にアクセス制限に直面すると、自ら制限を回避する方
法を見つけ出し、非公開のファイルにまでアクセスしたという。この事例は、AIが与えられた目
的を達成する過程で、許可された範囲を超えてシステムにアクセスしうることを示した。 
サイバー攻撃の脅威は、海の向こうだけの話ではない。日本でも9月下旬以降、物流、交通、
小売、カーシェアリングなど幅広い業種の企業が、不正アクセスによる個人情報流出の可能性を
相次いで公表している。これらの攻撃にAIが用いられたかどうかは明らかになっていないが、サ
イバー攻撃の影響が業種を問わず及んでいることは確かだ。政府も10月1 日から、能動的サイバ

### Page 2

2/6 
ー防御の一環として、重大なサイバー攻撃を未然に防ぐため、警察や自衛隊が攻撃元のサーバー
等にアクセスし、無害化措置を講じることを可能にした。 
AIがサイバー攻撃を自動化・高度化させる時代が到来しつつある。企業や政府の防御需要は急
増し、サイバーセキュリティ企業には特需がもたらされると期待される。個人投資家の間の「AI
の次なる恩恵銘柄はサイバー株」という見方にも、一定の説得力がある。しかし、 「サイバー株な
ら何でも上がる」と考えるのは早計である。AIによる攻撃の自動化・高度化は、攻撃件数を増や
すだけでなく、従来のセキュリティ製品や防御手法の有効性そのものを揺るがしかねないから
だ。本稿では、AIの攻撃能力の現在地と、それがサイバーセキュリティ企業の業績および株価に
与える影響を、データに基づいて検証する。そのうえで、 「サイバー株＝安全確実な成長株」とい
う見方をどう見直すべきか、個人投資家に向けて投資戦略を考えるための新たな視点を提示した
い。

2. AIの攻撃能力急伸とサイバーセキュリティ企業の業績・株価への影響 
(1) AIは人間なしでどこまで攻撃できるのか 
AIエージェントのサイバー攻撃関連能力は急速に高まっている。スタンフォード大学人間中心
AI研究所（Stanford HAI）が公表した「AI Index Report 2026」によれば、AIは人間が逐次指示
を与えなくても、専門的なサイバー課題を自律的に解ける水準に達しつつある。 
同レポートで紹介されている「Cybench」は、暗号、Webセキュリティ、リバースエンジニアリ
ング、フォレンジック、エクスプロイトなど、40の専門レベルのサイバーセキュリティ課題を評
価するベンチマークである。資料1 は、人間のガイドなし（unguided）でAIが課題を解決できた
割合の推移を示している。これを見ると、2024年時点ではわずか15％であった解決率が、2025年
には 82％へと急伸し、2026年には93％に達している。2年間で 78ポイントの上昇であり、AIが
自律的にサイバー課題を解く能力が大きく向上していることがわかる。

### Page 3

3/6 
資料 1 AI エージェントの自律的なサイバー課題解決能力は急伸している 
（注）サイバーセキュリティのベンチマーク「Cybench」における、人間のガイドなし（unguided）での課題解決率（％）の推移。
Cybench は暗号、Web セキュリティ、リバースエンジニアリング、フォレンジック、エクスプロイトなど 40 の専門レベル課題を評価
するもの。 
（出所）Stanford HAI, “AI Index Report 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) サイバー需要は企業の売上・利益に届いているのか 
こうしたAIによるサイバー攻撃の脅威が高まるなか、防御側であるサイバーセキュリティ企業
への需要拡大が期待される。実際、その需要はすでに企業業績に表れている。 
Nasdaqが公表した「Q1 2026 HXRXL Cybersecurity Commentary」によれば、サイバーセキュリ
ティ関連銘柄で構成される「Nasdaq ISE Cyber Security Select Index（HXRXL） 」構成企業の
2026 年第1 四半期決算は極めて好調であった。同レポートによると、構成企業の売上高は前年同
期比で 11％増加し、純利益は同29.3％の増加を記録しており、売上高以上に純利益が大きく伸び
ていることが確認できる。さらに、市場予想に対する結果をまとめた資料2を見ても、売上高で
予想を上回った企業は指数ウェイトの92.2％を占め、純利益に至っては100％の企業が予想を上
回った。サイバーセキュリティ需要の強さは、売上高と利益の双方に表れている。

### Page 4

4/6 
資料 2 サイバーセキュリティ企業の業績は市場予想を大きく上回る 
（注）Nasdaq ISE Cyber Security Select Index（HXRXL）構成企業の 2026 年第 1 四半期決算の集計結果。市場予想に対する売上
高および純利益の予想超え・予想未達の企業数（指数ウェイトベース）、ならびに平均上振れ・下振れ率（％）を示す。 
（出所）Nasdaq, “Q1 2026 HXRXL Cybersecurity Commentary”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) それでもサイバー株は上がるのか 
業績が好調であれば株価も上昇すると考えるのが自然だが、 「AIによる攻撃高度化＝サイバー株
上昇」とは必ずしも言い切れない。AIは防御需要を増やす一方で、既存のサイバーセキュリティ
製品を陳腐化させるとの懸念も生んでいるからだ。 
国際決済銀行（BIS）が2026 年7月に公表したレポート「A Mythos moment? Frontier AI and 
cyber risk」は、このパラドックスを指摘している。2026年 4月にAnthropicが高度なサイバー攻
撃能力を持つAIモデル「Mythos」を発表した際、サイバーセキュリティ関連株は大きく下落し
た。資料3 は、Mythos発表前を0とした場合のサイバーセキュリティ関連株の累積異常リターン
（CAR）を示している。発表後、サイバーセキュリティETF（IHAK）や主要企業（CRWD、PANWな
ど）のCARは軒並みマイナスに転じ、一部の系列ではグラフ上で約−30％の大幅なマイナスを記録
した。BISは、この市場反応について「新しいAIモデルの登場により、既存のサイバーセキュリテ
ィ製品やサービスが陳腐化する（obsolescence）という投資家の懸念を反映している」との解釈
を紹介している。つまり、AIの進化はサイバーセキュリティ市場全体を拡大させる可能性が高い
ものの、同時にゲームチェンジを引き起こし、旧来の技術やサービスに依存する企業の競争力を
急速に奪うリスクを孕んでいる。

### Page 5

5/6 
資料 3 高度な AI モデルの発表後、サイバーセキュリティ関連株は下落した 
（注）2026 年 4 月の高度な AI モデル「Mythos」発表前後の、サイバーセキュリティ関連株の累積異常リターン（CAR、％）。発表前
を 0 として推移を示している。「Glasswing partners」は CrowdStrike（CRWD）および Palo Alto Networks（PANW）を指す。 
（出所）Bank for International Settlements, “BIS Bulletin No. 129: A Mythos moment? Frontier AI and cyber risk”をもとに第一ラ
イフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. AI時代のサイバー株投資～「陳腐化リスク」を回避する新戦略～ 
第 2 章で見てきたように、AI はサイバー攻撃能力を飛躍的に高めている。また、足元のサイバ
ーセキュリティ企業の業績も好調である。しかし、最先端のAIモデルが登場した際には、市場は
既存のサイバーセキュリティ製品の「陳腐化リスク」に敏感に反応し、関連株は大きく売り込ま
れた。ここからいえるのは、サイバーセキュリティ市場全体は今後も拡大が見込まれるものの、
企業間の「勝ち負け」はこれまで以上に鮮明になるということだ。こうした環境変化のなか、個
人投資家には従来の投資アプローチを根本から見直すことが求められる。 
第一に、サイバーセキュリティセクター全体を丸ごと買うアプローチ（テーマ型ETFやインデ
ックスファンドなど）には注意が必要である。市場全体が成長しても、AIの進化に取り残され、
陳腐化して淘汰される旧世代の企業をポートフォリオに抱え込むリスクが高まるからだ。足元の
業績や過去の市場シェアだけで安心することはできない。第二に、個別銘柄を選別する際は、 「AI
ネイティブ」なサイバーセキュリティ企業であるかどうかが最大の焦点となる。攻撃側がAI を駆
使する以上、防御側もAIをシステムの中心に据え、未知の脅威を自律的に検知・遮断できるプラ
ットフォームを持っていなければ対抗できない。レガシーな製品を販売している企業ではなく、
クラウドベースで膨大なデータをAI で解析し、防御機能を継続的に高度化できる企業を見極める
必要がある。第三に、サイバー防衛の投資対象を「セキュリティ専業企業」以外にも広げること
である。 
最先端のAI モデルを開発し、強固なクラウドインフラを持つ巨大テック企業（メガテック）
は、自社のAI を活用した防衛技術を、顧客向けの強力なセキュリティサービスとして提供し始め

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
ている。豊富な資金力とAI 開発力を持つメガテックを、サイバー防衛の担い手として評価し、ポ
ートフォリオの中核に据えることも、AI時代の投資戦略として有効な選択肢となるだろう。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices
card-acceptance costs that had previously been surcharged
– and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-
plus’ approach to pricing. To the extent they can pass on
these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was

strong enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal
that demand is still outstripping supply.
• This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages
growth has been moderate for some years, though the
above-average increase in award wages this year will add
a little. Not all firms will seek to pass these cost increases
on, but some will, including because they have limited
scope to absorb the cost increase into margins.
• A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it

limits the imagination away from finding alternative,
efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with
recent academic research suggesting that firms form
expectations and set prices according to what is
happening in their own industry and experience, while
putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central
bank credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many
consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when
supply shocks abound.

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused
considerable upset this month, even though it is a reversion
to historical and international practice. The card schemes
used to impose “no-surcharge” rules, until the RBA banned
these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The
turnaround represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its
longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people to
choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer

made sense in a largely cashless economy. In any case, least-
cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in
the hands of the merchants who actually bear the cost.
In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker
price, leaving the total cost to the consumer unchanged, just
more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers
have absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first
place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated that around

16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though,
some firms have lifted their prices by more than any reasonable
estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway
coffee has been the centre of consumer discontent.
Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main
instigator of this month’s slump in consumer sentiment. Petrol
‘Cost-Plus’ costs
Forecast changes

• A full update to our forecasts was published in our October
Market Outlook  on Thursday 15 October.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and
the before-and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week
shows that higher interest rates also contributed to the decline.
In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the
RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses passing on increased

costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to
make that increase stick – is exactly the kind of inflationary
environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been
subjected to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from
pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel to
water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which
for decades were a reliable source of cost reductions, are
nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins

intact, demonstrates that demand is not weak enough to
prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations
might be higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this
as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair
Work Commission did hand down an above-average increase in
award wages this year and this was probably passed through
to prices in hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base

wages growth has been moderate for some years. Business
surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue
lately. This is why we were puzzled by the passage in the RBA
minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported
in business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate
consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU) has risen. It is
a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to
overshoot your forecasts, as the latest labour market data have

done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.
Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do
not have the market power, though coffee may be an exception
because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And
as noted above, large firms with everyday-low-price strategies
will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply chain.
Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on.
Some observers might see this as corporate greed, but another

way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality.
“Entrenched expectations that cost increases
can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against,
but only indirectly."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
It turns out that cost-plus pricing is especially common in
areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated
a lot in recent years.
There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The

failures of so many building firms a few years ago, squeezed
between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and
fixed-price contracts, were a case in point. Normally, there is
a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect
firms never to pass cost increases through to prices.
The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits
the imagination away from finding responses that might save

costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost
efficiency. It was equally limiting in times of soft demand: in
the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a belief
that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they
could not possibly accept cost increases, including in wages.
Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be
consistent with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle
of a central bank governor complaining that wages growth

was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to
economise on it and thereby boost productivity?
A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of
supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing as the ‘inflation
expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation,
but it is related. The stronger the tendency towards cost-plus
pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the

distinction matters. The recent academic literature includes
some fascinating empirical evidence  from New Zealand that
suggests that beliefs about current and future overall inflation
have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are
shaped less by economy-wide inflation developments than by
what is happening to their costs and in their industry.
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot

like firms (in aggregate) setting prices according to expected
inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something
quite different is going on. If firms’ beliefs about future
economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing
decisions, then neither does the credibility of a central bank’s
commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism to
get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand
enough to convince firms that cost-plus will not fly in the

market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers
or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when supply shocks
abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade
or even self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that
happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.
The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can
be reduced. That is more likely to be a question for regulators
than for central banks.

THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
bias towards an October or December decision. Williams
stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in
this cycle given known information. And Waller also showed
confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are

doing some of the FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest
rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating that
one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation
near the 2.0%yr medium-term target. However, as discussed
in this month’s Market Outlook , capacity pressures are likely
to see inflation consistently above target, rather than below,
in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity in the
housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to

remain modestly restrictive over the forecast horizon and will
also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade
account. At USD133.5bn, the September result was a whisker
inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More
impressively, throughout 2025 and 2026 year-to-date the
surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the
average of 2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth
of export growth is as impressive as the headline annual gain of

36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese
investment in neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time,
both trade and income receipts from offshore investment should
buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound
foreign investment should moderate the pace of gains against
other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the
evolution of producer prices across Asia, and the likelihood

of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent
decades continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was
one of the key drivers of declining goods prices. While
still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing
development to instead be driven by capital, technology and
energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking
production are also increasingly prioritising resilience, security
and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore more

probably future supply-chain developments will help contain
rising costs through productivity than drive sustained price
declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September
Labour Force Survey . Employment surprised to the
downside, –39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force
participation from August’s near-historic high largely offset.
The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August

was revised up.
Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless,
the take home point from the update is that the labour
market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs
below working-age population growth. We expect the
unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter
financial conditions and cost-of-living pressures restrict
activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast
peak is near at 4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the

communication challenge for the RBA. The September minutes
were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing
on materialising upside risks to inflation. But, with the
unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption
of 4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the
labour market is becoming harder to defend. Still, with a clear
focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our
near-term CPI forecasts in the October Market Outlook  – we

still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in
September, taking the annual rate up to 3.7%yr. Energy prices
were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation
was a touch above consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr).
Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months
(0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4% (3.1%yr) on a

0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport
services and a 0.3% increase in medical care services (2.7%yr
and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release
of the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures,
with the headline and core measures up 6.3%yr and 5.0%yr
respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these
developments, firms across multiple districts citing tariffs,
insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost

pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect
further price increases as contracts/hedges roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is
enough signal in these reports to warrant a follow-up hike
at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee
members believe they are near the end of the tightening cycle
and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman
both showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Downward revisions to 2027 outlook
This week we have updated our New Zealand economic
outlook for the next year, in particular reflecting the latest
developments in the energy market and key financial
variables (long-term interest rates and the exchange rate).

After last week’s marking down of our forecast for through-
year GDP growth in 2026 to 2.5%yr, this week we have
reduced our forecast of growth in 2027 by 0.3ppts to 2.7%yr.
This downward revision reflects a less positive assessment
of the outlooks for consumption and investment (including
residential investment) considering the more elevated path
assumed for energy prices and a higher near-term track for
long-term interest rates (as discussed in our updated Market

Outlook, published earlier this week).
A partial offset to weaker growth in domestic demand is
provided by a slightly more positive outlook for exports
(particularly exports of manufactured goods and tourism
inflows) because of a downward revision to our expectations
for the NZ dollar. We now expect NZD/USD to end this
year around current levels at 0.56 – 3c lower than expected
previously – before appreciating to around 0.64 over the
course of 2027 as interest rate differentials with the US

gradually close and as the USD appreciates. We think that the
low value of the NZ dollar – in trade-weighted terms now at a
17-year low – helps to explain the recent strong performance
in the previously moribund manufacturing sector, as well as
tourist inflows from key markets such as Australia.
Last week we raised our forecast for CPI inflation at year end
to 4.3%yr, largely on account of recent developments in global
energy prices (although the weak NZ dollar has compounded the

effect). This week we have slightly revised up our forecast for
inflation for the first half of 2027. But because energy prices are
assumed to decline in 2027 from what is now a higher starting
level, our 2027 year-end forecast for inflation has nudged
down 0.1ppts to 1.6%yr. Needless to say, forecasts of headline
inflation, in particular, are subject to significant revision given the
uncertain outlook for the Middle East conflict and uncertainty

about how that will be reflected in global energy prices.
Ahead of next week’s very important Q3 CPI report – which
could prove decisive – we have made no changes to our
forecast for the RBNZ’s policy settings. So, for now we
continue to think that despite the higher near-term inflation
outlook the RBNZ will stick with the strategy communicated
in the September Monetary Policy Statement, i.e. that at the
vv28 October meeting it will pause to assess the impact of this

year’s 50bps of policy tightening, before considering a further
upward adjustment in December.
Turning to this week’s data, the news from the housing market
remained decidedly downbeat. House sales fell a further 4% in
September – the seventh consecutive month of decline – to be
12% lower than a year earlier. With the stock of listings at an
11-year high, the median selling time remains elevated. And
so unsurprisingly, house prices fell for a fourth consecutive

month and are now down 1.6%yr. Prices remain especially
soft in the North Island, with prices down more than 3%yr in
Auckland and more than 4%yr in Wellington. Given upward
pressures on mortgage rates, broader strength in the economy
– including a stronger labour market – will likely be required to
reverse this trend. On that score, MBIE’s measure of online job
advertisements was little changed in September, continuing
the flat trend since the beginning of this year.

In other news, retail spending, as measured by electronic card
payments, decreased 0.4%m/m in September following an
unrevised 0.9% decline in August. This was a weaker result than
we had expected based on the recent sawtooth pattern and
our own internal data. We should treat these data with some
suspicion given that for quite a while now the Stats NZ series
seems not to have reflected the spending seen through banks’
own card spending networks, perhaps suggesting changes in the

payments arena may be leaving the official sector data behind.
Even so, unsurprisingly, with petrol prices rebounding late in
the month, spending on fuel increased 2.9%mth. However,
core spending, which excludes spending on fuel and vehicles,
fell 0.7%m/m. While spending on consumables (groceries)
increased modestly in September, this was at the expense of
spending on ‘nice to haves’. Spending on hospitality suffered a
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

further 2.3%mth fall following a 1.9%mth decline in August,

Despite the weak outcome for the August and September

months, retail spending over the September quarter still
eked out a minor 0.1% gain, while core spending increased
0.4%. Given higher prices, at face value this points to a weak
quarter for volumes (next week’s CPI report will cast more
light on pricing behaviour during the quarter). It is worth
noting that total retail spending, as will be depicted in the
official quarterly retail sales survey released next month, could
point to somewhat stronger growth. Purchases of big-ticket

items that often are bought on credit – such as cars, furniture
and appliances – are underrepresented in the electronic card
spending survey.
Given subdued domestic demand, local hospitality businesses
have relied on foreign visitors to drive growth in spending.
And the good news is that visitor arrivals increased to a new
post-Covid high in August to be up 8%yr. Arrivals of Chinese
citizens have grown by almost a third to sit near pre-Covid
levels in recent months, while arrivals of US citizens grew

17%yr. However, the Middle East conflict has impacted
long-haul arrivals from Europe (UK arrivals are down 8%yr).
Meanwhile, inflows of migrants remain at modest levels,
totalling around 20,000 in the year to August – about half
what might be expected on average over the cycle.
News from the manufacturing sector remained positive in
September, with the BusinessNZ Manufacturing PMI rising
to a 3-month high of 56.5 in September. Improvement was
seen across all subcomponents of the index, with the new

orders index rising 1.8pts to 57.0. We think that the recovery in
manufacturing this year owes much to the weakness of the NZ
dollar – especially against the Australian dollar – and activity
associated with a strong domestic primary sector and an
emerging recovery in domestic residential construction activity.
Last but certainly not least, ahead of next week’s all-important
Q3 CPI, the Selected Prices report for September was firmer
than expected. That was mainly due to larger than expected

increases in airfares and holiday accommodation costs, which
offset softer than expected food prices.
Accounting for the September prices data, we’ve left our
forecast for Q3 inflation  at 0.7%. That would see the annual
inflation rate dropping back to 3.7%, down from 4.1% in the
year to June. Underlying that move has been a moderation in
fuel prices after they rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle
East war. That’s balanced against increases in council rates,

building costs and food prices.
Our forecast is lower than the RBNZ’s September MPS
forecast for a 0.8% rise (+3.9%yr). However, with some large
swings in volatile items like fuel, the RBNZ will likely focus
on measures of core inflation. Core inflation was already at
firm levels in the June quarter, with key measures sitting in
the upper part of the RBNZ’s target band. We expect that
they will take another step higher in September, to sit around

3%. Upside surprises in core inflation would be an important
development for the RBNZ ahead of the October OCR review,
as this could suggest that the lower NZD and higher oil prices
are boosting underlying price pressures by more and sooner
than they may have expected.

### Page 7

Q3 is likely to produce a very similar outcome to Q2, with 
quarterly growth of around 1.0% holding the annual rate at 
4.3%yr. Year-to-date, growth will remain around 4.7%ytd 
thanks to the Q1 result.
The detail and partial data for September will again highlight 
the weak state of the consumer and domestic-oriented 
businesses, but also the incredible strength of exports. With 
the trade surplus back at record highs, there are limits to the 
contribution possible from net exports hence, necessitating 
a strengthening of domestic demand if aggregate growth is 
to continue at the rate authorities intend into 2027. Expect 
additional stimulus through the turn of the year.

## Document 247: Bank bond supply to stay at cruising altitude in 2027

Source PDF: all report pdf/Bank bond supply to stay at cruising altitude in 2027.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027.
Redemptions remain the main driver of the high supply, but
the modest macroeconomic outlook is not expected to push
supply up any further. We foresee covered bond issuance
reaching €185bn, senior unsecured bond supply at €210bn and
another €55bn in subordinated bank bonds to be printed

Marine Leleux and Jesse Norcross
High redemption volumes are expected to keep EUR bank bond supply
elevated in 2027
Variables driving next year's bank bond
supply
We expect EUR bank bond supply to reach €450bn in 2027, down
slightly from the €455bn forecast for 2026. In our view, several
variables will be steering next year’s issuance.
Redemptions will remain the central driver of the elevated 2027
bank bond supply forecast. Nearly €400bn of bonds mature in
2027, up €30bn year-on-year and the highest level in a decade.

### Page 2

The increase is especially signiﬁcant in the covered bond segment
where €186bn will mature, a historic high.
The subordinated segment will also rise, with redemptions totalling
€45bn, up about €6bn from 2026. Of this total, €31bn is in Tier 2
instruments and €14bn in AT1s. The senior unsecured segment will
partially oﬀset these increases, with total redemptions reaching
more than €162bn, down from more than €170bn recorded this
year. The majority stems from senior bail-in bonds (senior non-

preferred or senior HoldCo) with more than €90bn and an
additional €71bn maturing in senior preferred instruments.
Despite diverging trends across segments, elevated redemption
volumes remain the main driver of bank issuance in the euro bond
primary market, as institutions reﬁnance maturing instruments.

While high redemption levels point to stronger supply in 2027,
other factors also play a role, such as the macroeconomic
environment. Eurozone GDP growth is forecast to increase slightly in
the second half of next year to reach 1.5%, still well below levels
seen after the pandemic.
Furthermore, our economists pencil in an additional European
Central Bank rate hike in 2026, bringing policy rates to 2.75% in
early 2027. The higher interest rate environment is not expected to

Asset swap spread levels initially proved very resilient to

geopolitical instability and related economic changes this year.
Despite some volatility in March, following the outbreak of the US-
Israel conﬂict with Iran, spread levels recovered quickly and reached
levels close to their historic low. However, a broader escalation of
the conﬂict across the Middle East, coupled with the absence of a
clear path to resolution, could put further pressure on spreads.
In addition, budget and political concerns in France and OAT-Bund

volatility, as well as broader European ﬁscal concerns, have recently
led to spreads widening again. The combination of French political
uncertainty and the Middle East conﬂict could trigger some periods
of volatility in the market in 2027. However, we believe these
uncertainties will not be a strong negative for bank bond supply.
Depending on the timing, though, periodic widening may make
ﬁnancial institutions less keen to pre-fund redemptions coming up

in 2028.

Our estimate includes €185bn in covered bonds, comprising
€180bn in benchmark-sized issuances, including ﬂoating rate
notes, and €5bn in sub-benchmark bonds. This is a €5bn drop
versus our 2026 forecast. It reﬂects the pre-ﬁnancing already made
by issuers this year in preparation for the historically high
redemptions.
Furthermore, next year’s mortgage lending forecasts point to
stagnation, somewhat limiting any further increase in covered

bond issuance. Besides, with the securitisation package entering its
ﬁnalisation stage, issuers with high funding needs may shift some
secured issuance towards securitisation for diversiﬁcation
purposes. Increasing demand for covered bonds from overseas
investors driven by regulatory developments may also result in
more currency diversiﬁcation, away from EUR-denominated
issuance.
We forecast senior unsecured supply to reach €210bn next year.
This is split between €70bn in senior preferred bonds and €140bn in

senior bail-in instruments. It represents a €10bn YoY decline in

### Page 5

senior preferred supply, reﬂecting both the drop in the segment’s
redemptions from 2027 onwards and the potential impact of the
Crisis Management and Deposit Insurance (CMDI) framework. As the
CMDI reforms are expected to enter into force in 2028, they could
reduce the attractiveness of senior preferred bonds versus senior
bail-in instruments in some EU countries, as senior preferred
exposures will rank lower than, and no longer pari passu to,
uncovered deposits in the creditor hierarchy. The funding cost

advantage of senior preferred unsecured issuance could decline. At
the same time, issuing senior non-preferred bonds could serve the
purpose of increasing the bail-inable layer protecting preferred
senior bonds and their current ratings. This could shift some
issuers’ focus towards the senior non-preferred bucket instead of
the senior preferred segment.
Our forecast for senior non-preferred supply remains stable
compared to our 2026 estimate. While redemptions will increase by

€6bn in 2027 and further in 2028 to reach €102bn, we believe the
impact of higher reﬁnancing needs on non-preferred senior supply
will be partially oﬀset by the lack of balance sheet growth against
the backdrop of higher interest rates. Furthermore, the
outperformance of US dollar debt versus EUR-denominated debt
could see issuers turn more often towards the USD market,
potentially negatively aﬀecting EUR senior bail-in supply.
Lastly, we expect Tier 2 supply to reach €35bn and AT1 supply to

reach €20bn in 2027. This is a €5bn increase YoY in both AT1 and
Tier 2 compared to our 2026 forecast, mainly driven by higher
redemption levels. With AT1 redemptions in 2027 increasing by
€3bn YoY to €14bn, net supply is likely to be marginally higher next
year. In Tier 2, redemptions increase by €4bn to €31bn, essentially
meaning that net supply will only be marginally higher.
As the chart below highlights, net supply in AT1 has been
signiﬁcant in recent years but has slowed this year as the focus has

shifted towards reﬁnancing. As such, while we expect more AT1
supply next year compared to 2026, the levels are relatively in line
with the longer-term trend.

The main driver of supply will also be reﬁnancing for subordinated
capital. We expect a further marginal decline in CET1 buﬀers,
although most banks are already operating at target AT1 and Tier 2
ratios. Still, there are some issuers with substantial gaps in either
AT1 or Tier 2 ratios, which they may look to improve, leading to a

slight increase in supply.
Signiﬁcant Risk Transfer (SRT) issuance has also increased in recent
years and will continue to grow as a tool for banks in terms of
balance sheet and capital management, while it is also growing as
an investable asset class. Although SRTs provide capital and RWA
relief, they do not replace the gone-concern and loss-absorption
capacity of AT1 and Tier 2 instruments. As such, they play a more
complementary role in capital management, and we do not expect

it to have a major impact on bank capital issuance in 2027.
Bank M&A could also be a wildcard for 2027 issuance. With several
large M&A transactions potentially taking place, this may lead to
some capital optimisation and less incremental supply needs;
conversely, goodwill eﬀects, potential regulatory increases in buﬀer
requirements or RWA inﬂation may lead to a slight increase in the
need for capital. However, on the whole, we would expect M&A to

have a slight dampening eﬀect on supply.

## Document 363: Sales Trader Commentary_ TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally_ _ GSTW

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sales Trader Commentary_ TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally_ _ GSTW.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-13
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Sales Trader Commentary: TAIEX 49k Wobble -
July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa
Rally? | GSTW
13 October 2026 | 4:11 PM Taiwan Standard Time
Robert Wang
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C. Taipei Branch
FICC & Equities
Index lost the 49k points threshold and saw the biggest decline on a point basis since
the end of July. Market seemed to lose the steam to break through 50k points after
the past choppy sessions. Is this July de ja vu or some healthy pullback? Foreigners

and domestic investors seem to share different narrative. Check out below for our
investor feedback.
Key Investor Feedback and Observations
Positioning. we saw meaningful outflows today from all kinds of investors today,
again focusing in ex-TSMC large caps. Today foreigners net sold US$5.2bn today,
the second largest in the history (simultaneously drove TWD weakness over 32.23
today). Domestic investors, especially retail, on the other hand, anchored the

market with the record high margin loan balance (as of 10/12/2026). Local funds
remained buyer as well mainly thanks to ETFs inflow.
Macro. CPI headline brought uncertainty and forced global investors to turn
cautious, which was exacerbated by high yields and crude. For Taiwan, AI trade is
relatively resilient to the Middle Eastern tension and high energy prices. However,
at some point, it is going to have a real economic impact either through Fed or

some other macro data.
Sentiment. Surprisingly, people are still bullish in Taiwan, though they also
acknowledge some choppy trading sessions ahead. Local investor feedback is not
calling for capitulation; in fact, most believe this is just another "two-steps forward
one-step backward" cadence towards another record high. Nonetheless, some
retail start taking chips off the table. This echoes our observation among the
foreign investors. Yes, the AI fundamental future is all sunshine, but as we head into

the end of year, it is natural for them to protect their great fun thus far this year
instead of adding on immediate risk. I'd imagine the next few weeks are going to be
full of 1) rapid sector rotation; 2) weak or unsustainable momentum before Santa
comes into town after the midterm election for our year-end rally Christmas gift.
TSMC Earnings and Others. We know the numbers are going to be good, and we
also know people are going to sell-on-facts after TSMC earnings call; recall most

recent one happened right after the meeting that scarred a lot of investors since.
Perhaps this time people started trimming ahead (and maybe too early?), which
provides some window for a quick rebound into small and mid cap. OCP summit is
happening right now, and TPCA Show will start next week. Watch out for single
stock movement mentioned in the conferences.
10/13/26, 4:13 PM Sales Trader Commentary: TAIEX 49k Wobble - July De Ja Vu, or Just Pausing Before the Santa Rally? | GSTW

https://marquee.gs.com/s/content/markets/812c043a-8535-4003-887e-b01f79096974 1/8

### Page 2

Sector. 1) ABF/CCL is still the crowded long sectors for both foreign and domestic
investor, but echoing on above points, investors need more catalysts to add on
risks or drive the re-ratings. 2) Power is another big issues that data centers need
to resolve, power solutions and fuel cells name are also key focus in Taiwan. 3) Let's
not forget about OSATs. We need more equipment and testing capacity to suffice
enlarging demand from both ASIC and GPU.

Key Stocks I Follow: Unimicron, Kinsus, NYPCB, ZDT, EMC, TUC, Delta, Lite-On,
Kaori, ASE, KYEC, All Ring
Risk. Upside looks thin in the short term. Good earnings are not enough - look at
Nanya Tech and Memory sector today. Chasing momentum now may get hurt. I also
smelled some short unwind today (i.e. funding short names outperformance such
as Realtek, Novatek, Shipper, etc) - signs of de-leveraging. Directionless is how I
would describe the past few days, but maybe it's time to find some value Buy?

Max loss amount invested is 100%

performance over such longer periods of time can differ significantly from the stated multiple of the performance (or the inverse of the
performance) of their underlying index or benchmark during the same period of time. This effect can be magnified in volatile markets.

## Document 369: Sukuk_ Structure, stability and strategic opportunity (for Private Bank only)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Sukuk_ Structure, stability and strategic opportunity (for Private Bank only).pdf

Islamic finance is growing at a rapid pace, driven 
primarily by increasing demand for investors who 
seek Shariah -compliant investments. Sukuk, often 
referred to as Islamic bonds, surpassed USD 1trn in 
2025, making them one of the fastest -growing 
segments o f Islamic finance. The asset class’s 
unique value proposition is driving its popularity, 
especially in an increasingly volatile global  
economic landscape. 
In this publication, we will explore the evolving role of Sukuk in wealth management, 
examining market fundamentals, risk frameworks and emerging opportunities within 
this market.

### Page 3

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  3

Sukuk, commonly referred to as Islamic bonds, are Shariah-compliant investment 
certificates representing ownership interests in underlying assets, usufructs (the 
right to use an asset and receive its benefits, without owning it) or ventures. In 
return, Sukuk holders receive periodic distributions, typically in the form of rental 
payments (Ijarah), profit-sharing (Mudarabah) or asset-generated cash flows, and 
upon maturity the principal amount is repaid. Sukuk differ from conventional 
bonds in several ways: 
Fig. 1  
Sukuk vs. conventional bonds

Aspect  Conventional bonds Sukuk

Core principle  Lending (time-value of money) Asset ownership 
Underlying asset  Any asset, business or project Must be Sharia-compliant 
Returns  Guaranteed (fixed or floating interest) Not legally guaranteed (depends on 
underlying asset’s performance) 
Risk   Issuer bears all performance risk Shared risk between parties 
Transparency  Moderate (depends on disclosure) High (asset identification required)

Source: Standard Chartered

The role of Sukuk in the investing landscape is evolving amid 
global uncertainty and rising geopolitical tensions. Investors 
are increasingly seeking more stable and asset ‑backed 
alternatives. An investor can consider investing in Sukuk for 
the following reasons: 
Resilient 
Sukuk provide asset -backed collateral that conventional 
bonds often lack, offering a cushion during volatile market 
conditions. Historically, Islamic investments, including Sukuk’, 
have been countercyclical, i.e. they tend to expand or remain 
stable when c onventional markets contract. Across major 
market stress from the 2018 US-China Trade War through the 
2026 Middle East Conflict, Sukuk bonds consistently 
outperformed Global Bonds — delivering smaller drawdowns 
in risk-off episodes such as the 2018 US-China trade war and 
2022 higher for longer inflation, and greater resilience in 
geopolitical oil shocks, underscoring the asset class's 
structural defensive characteristics.

1 The evolving role of Sukuk

Strong fundamentals
The Sukuk market experienced an unprecedented 25% growth in issuance in 2025, with issuance nearly USD 300bn. According
to some  reports,  the  Sukuk  market  could  reach  USD  3trn  by  2030  driven  by  increased  demand  for  Shariah-compliant
investments, sovereign funds’ diversification needs, development of regulatory frameworks for Sukuk and growth in Middle East

capital markets.  Nearly  80%  of  Fitch ‑rated Sukuk  carry  investment ‑grade ratings  and  have  little  or  no  history  of  default,
underscoring the sector’s strong fundamentals. These factors should help to instil confidence among investors, who can look at
Sukuk as central building blocks of their portfolio.

Convergence with sustainable investing trends
Sukuk and sustainable bonds share more common ground than their different origins suggest, making sustainable Sukuk a
natural bridge between Islamic finance and sustainable investing. This also helps explain Sukuk’s growing appeal beyond
traditional Muslim investors, as evidenced by Sustainable Sukuk issuance rising from USD 464mn in 2017 to USD 9bn in 2024,

representing a CAGR of 52.7%. The overlap starts with what Sukuk exclude: Shariah principles prohibit interest, excessive
uncertainty and speculation, and screen out sectors financing harmful industries such as tobacco, gambling, weapons, adult
entertainment and alcohol – a negative screen that closely aligns with the exclusionary (negative screening) method, commonly
used in sustainable investing. Tradi tional Sukuk bonds require proceeds to be linked to identifiable real assets or projects,

creating a ring-fenced structure like use-of-proceeds sustainable bonds. Combined with Shariah’s broader objectives of social
welfare, resource stewardship and equita ble risk-sharing, this creates strong alignment with the United Nations Sustainable
Development Goals.
Green Sukuk is an established example, where proceeds are earmarked for environmental projects such as renewable energy,
clean transport, energy-efficient buildings or sustainable water and wastewater management. Issuers must meet both Shariah

approval from a recognised scholarly board and alignment with International Capital Market Association (ICMA) Green Bond
Principles.
Traditional Sukuk already comply with Shariah laws such as avoiding Riba (interest -based returns arising from lending
transactions), Gharar (investments with ambiguity) and Maysir (speculation). Sustainable Sukuk build on this foundation by
adding environmental and social objectives. The table below highlights how sustainable Sukuk differ from traditional Sukuk.

Fig. 4
Traditional Sukuk vs. Sustainable bonds vs Sustainable Sukuk
Aspect Traditional Sukuk Sustainable Bonds Sustainable Sukuk
Underlying structure Asset-linked
Use of proceeds For any Shariah-compliant

Digital integration
The integration  of  blockchain  technology  and  digital  platforms  is  revolutionising  Sukuk  issuance  and  trading,  dramatically
reducing transaction costs and expanding accessibility to  a broader  investor base.  For instance, SAKKEX (an  independent
blockchain-based Sukuk marketplace) streamlines drafting, issuance, and near ‑instant settlement. Its Sukuk Wallet App also
allows investors to trade on the go. Several such digital platforms now allow tokenised Sukuk that enable fractional ownership,

automated Shariah  compliance  verification  and  real-time  settlement,  thereby  addressing  challenges  that  have  persisted  in
Islamic finance markets. Digital tools also simplify cross border Sukuk transactions by enabling end to end digital origination and
trading, with smart contracts automating profit sharing to cut operational costs and enhance transparency.

### Page 6

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026 6
Sukuk structures define the nature of ownership, risk -sharing and return
generation. Several types of Sukuk structures exist and are defined by the
underlying Shariah contract. We discuss some prominent ones below and see
how they align with various phases of an investor’s wealth journey:
Sukuk al-Ijarah is a Shariah-compliant lease-based bond where the investor gains partial ownership in a physical

asset such as a building, airport or equipment. The asset is then leased back to the issuer who pays a nominal
(pre-agreed) rent to the investor. An investor seeking stable, predictable income streams with lower volatility can
consider investing in these Sukuk. The original principal amount is returned at the end of the lease period.
Ijarah Sukuk are ideal for the wealth preservation phase of an investor’s wealth journey. For instance, investors
nearing retirement typically need to restructure their portfolios by shifting from growth to preservation while

generating steady income to cover living expenses. Ijarah Sukuk deliver on both fronts: protecting capital through
asset-backed structures while providing predictable rental income that supports household needs without
depleting principal.
Sukuk al-Murabahah are cost-plus financing structures where the issuer buys an asset and immediately resells
it to the investors at a marked-up price, with payment deferred over a pre-determined time. Consider this: Let us

assume, a company needs USD 100m. A special purpose vehicle (SPV), established by the company, mobilises
funds from investors and buys commodities worth USD 100m and immediately resells them to the company for
USD 112m payable over time. The USD 12m difference is the agreed profit margin. Murabahah Sukuk ar e best
suited for investors seeking income over a short to medium term, typically, 1-5 years. Investments in this type of
Sukuk return the principal gradually throughout the investment period rather than only at maturity.

Investments in Murabahah Sukuk are best suited for wealth protection and income generation. Unlike Ijarah which
return principal as a lump sum at maturity, Murabahah amortise both profit and principal in each instalment. This
makes them ideal for retirees who need to systematically draw down capital to fund living expenses without
depleting their portfolio too quickly.
Sukuk al-Mudarabah is a profit-sharing partnership arrangement where investors provide 100% of the capital

and operate as silent partners. The issuer manages the business or project as the active partner. Under a
Mudarabah arrangement, financial losses arising from normal bu siness risks are borne by the investors in
proportion to their capital contribution, while the Mudarib loses its effort and entitlement to profit. However, where
losses result from negligence, misconduct, breach of agreed terms, or misconduct by the Mudari b, the Mudarib
may be held liable. Think of it as venture capital meets bonds; you fund the business, professionals run it and

you share the profits or bear the losses resulting from the business activities. Mudarabah Sukuk are often
regarded as one of the  structures most closely aligned with the Shariah principle of profit and loss sharing.
However, they bear higher risk and lower predictability compared to other structures.
Investors with a growth mindset and open to high -risk tolerance can consider investing in these Sukuk . They
best align with accumulation and growth phase of the wealth journey, typically, when investors have long time

horizons to absorb volatility and can afford capital risk for higher returns. However, to balance risk, it is important
for investors to diversify across various types of Sukuk. Even within Mudarabah, they can consider investing
in multiple projects, sectors and geographies.
1 Sukuk structures and
wealth mandates

### Page 7

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  7

A joint venture partnership structure where both investors and the issuer contribute capital is Sukuk al -
Musharakah. In this structure, both parties (issuer and investors) jointly provide capital, participate in managing 
the venture, and share profits or losses based on agreed upon ratios. This structure appeals to investors focused 
on growth, interested in active participation, and prepared to take on greater risk in pursuit of higher returns. 
Musharakah Sukuk are best suited for the growth and accumulation phase where investors are building wealth 
aggressively, comfortable with high risk and able to lock up capital for the long term (typically 5+ years).

Sukuk al-Wakalah is an agency arrangement where investors appoint a representative from the issuing company 
as their Wakil (agent) to invest their funds in specific Shariah-compliant assets or projects in exchange for a fee. 
This arrangement is similar to a mutual fund or ETF, where the agent manages a diversified pool of Shariah 
compliant assets on behalf of the investors. Investors benefit from professional fund management, diversification, 
transparency and flexibility. Depending on issuer preferences, Wakalah Sukuk can b e structured with lower 
investment thresholds, making them accessible to a wide range of investors. 
Wakalah Sukuk suitability depends heavily on the underlying asset allocation and risk profile. Conservative 
Wakalah structures investing in stable, income -generating assets suit wealth preservation. Moderate -risk 
Wakalah can complement accumulation portfolios as a diversification tool, though not as a primary growth driver 
as their structure and risk mandate limit upside potential.

Sukuk al-Istisna is a project financing structure based on a manufacturing or construction contract. Investors 
provide upfront capital to finance the construction of specific assets. Upon completion, the asset is sold to an end 
buyer (often the original issuer or a third party) who makes deferred payments including the cost plus a mark-up, 
which are then distributed to investors. Istisna suit seasoned investors with construction finance expertise and 
those who can absorb development risk and commit capital for the long term. Owing to its complexity and higher 
risk profile, this type of Sukuk typically requires large initial investments, making it less accessible to retail 
investors.  
In the wealth cycle, Istisna are best aligned with the accumulation and growth phase, when long time horizons 
can absorb construction delays and development risks. The long-term commitment and potential for cost overruns 
or project failure make it unsuitable for later wealth stages such as retirement.

Sukuk al -Salam is an Islamic bond backed by a forward purchase contract where investors pay upfront for 
commodities to be delivered later. At its core, they are similar to forward contracts. However, unlike a forward 
contract, Salam contracts are fully prepaid, standardi sed and designed with clearly defined delivery terms to 
reduce uncertainty (gharar). Under this arrangement, an investor profits from the difference between the lower 
Salam purchase price (paid today) and the higher resale price (received at delivery).  
Salam Sukuk carry high delivery and price risk and aligns well with investors in the growth and accumulation 
phase. Salam requires strong commodity‑market expertise, deep understanding of production and delivery risks, 
and the ability to assess the issuer’s operational capacity to deliver the specified goods on time.

Owing to their ethical framework and asset -backed nature, Sukuk are often
considered relatively less risky than conventional bonds. However, they are not
immune to risks. Unique challenges such as asset quality deterioration and
structure-specific exposures require rigorous risk management.
